# 🧪 Week 19.5 – Challenge Lab: Mastering ZAP in DevSecOps Pipelines

---

## 🎯 Goal

Apply what you've learned about OWASP ZAP and DAST to:

- Simulate authenticated attacks
- Integrate ZAP into your app lifecycle
- Fine-tune findings and thresholds
- Automate enforcement in real-world pipelines

You’ll demonstrate full-stack security testing maturity in your CI/CD flow.

---

## 🧩 Challenge Tasks

---

### ✅ 1. 🔐 Create a ZAP Context File

- Define your login endpoint (`/api/auth/login`)
- Use a **bearer token injection** or form-based session login
- Exclude logout or static files from scanning

📄 `zap/notestream.context`

```xml
<context>
  <authentication>
    <method type="script-based" />
    <loginurl>https://staging.notestreampro.dev/api/auth/login</loginurl>
    <bearertoken source="env" name="BEARER_TOKEN" />
  </authentication>
  <include>
    <url>https://staging.notestreampro.dev/.*</url>
  </include>
  <exclude>
    <url>.*logout.*</url>
    <url>.*static/.*</url>
  </exclude>
</context>
🧠 ZAP can now reuse this across scans to stay logged in and scan deeper.

✅ 2. 🕷️ Launch Full ZAP Spider + Active Scan in Pipeline
Use zap-full-scan.py or launch ZAP daemon + CLI separately

Capture and store the report artifacts

docker run --rm -v $(pwd):/zap/wrk \
  owasp/zap2docker-stable zap-full-scan.py \
  -t "$NOTE_STREAM_PRO_STAGING_URL" \
  -c zap/notestream.context \
  -r zap-auth-full.html \
  -J zap-auth-full.json
✅ 3. ✋ Fail Pipeline on Custom Risk Threshold
Write a CI/CD-safe parser:

📄 .zap/check-severity.sh

#!/bin/bash
THRESHOLD="Medium"

if jq -e --arg risk "$THRESHOLD" \
  '.site.alerts[] | select(.risk == $risk)' zap-auth-full.json > /dev/null; then
  echo "❌ Found $THRESHOLD or higher risk vulnerabilities"
  exit 1
else
  echo "✅ No risky vulnerabilities found"
fi
Add this as a build gate before promotion to production.

✅ 4. 📬 Push ZAP Report to Your Security Team Automatically
Upload to S3:

aws s3 cp zap-auth-full.html s3://org-security-reports/notestream/$(date +%s).html
Or send a Slack notification using webhook + ZAP summary (bonus).

✅ 5. 🔁 Rerun After Each Feature Merge to staging
Make ZAP run:

Every merge to staging

Every time infra or API changes

During security smoke tests

Use a GitHub/GitLab conditional trigger:

if: github.ref == 'refs/heads/staging'
🧠 Advanced Bonus (Optional)
Test with expired tokens

Add CSRF token simulation

Integrate with a dashboard like DefectDojo or Dependency-Track

Link ZAP to GitHub Security tab using SARIF format

✅ Completion Checklist
Item	Status
ZAP context file configured	✅
Authenticated scan with spider and active tests	✅
Pipeline fails on medium/high alerts	✅
Reports uploaded and traceable	✅
Automated re-scan policy implemented	✅

🧪 SCA Coverage in Our Curriculum
📅 Week	Module	Tool	What We Did
Week 6	CI/CD Pipelines Part I	Trivy, npm audit, bandit	Performed scans on code dependencies during builds (SCA + SAST)

✅ Recap of SCA-Related Actions
Generate SBOMs using Syft or Trivy

Scan dependencies in both Node.js and Python via:

npm audit

pip-audit

bandit for code-level issues

Enforce gating rules (e.g., fail build if trivy finds CVEs above a threshold)

Link scans to CI/CD and artifact promotion (only allow trusted, signed, and scanned artifacts)

❓ What's the Difference Between SCA and SAST/DAST?
Type	Focus	Tools
SCA	3rd-party dependencies (e.g., lodash, express, requests)	Trivy, Grype, npm audit, pip-audit
SAST	Source code analysis (your code)	Bandit, semgrep
DAST	Runtime behavior (live app testing)	ZAP, Burp, Postman (in some workflows)
So:

# 🔐 Week 19.5 – Security Review: Operationalizing DAST with ZAP

---

## 🎯 Overview

In this deep dive, you transitioned from “running ZAP manually” to **automating authenticated, risk-aware scans** within CI/CD — a critical skill for enterprise DevSecOps engineers.

You now treat ZAP as a **pipeline-native, version-aware security scanner** that enforces real-world policies.

---

## 🔐 Security Capabilities Achieved

| Capability | How it was implemented |
|------------|------------------------|
| ✅ Authenticated DAST | Token injection, context files, secure login scanning |
| ✅ Full-path scanning | Spidering and active scanning across your staging app |
| ✅ Alert-driven gating | Pipeline fails if Medium/High findings are detected |
| ✅ Artifact traceability | Reports uploaded and versioned with each build |
| ✅ Continuous enforcement | Triggers on every merge to `staging` or on-demand |

---

## 🧠 Why This Matters in Enterprise DevSecOps

| Real-World Risk | How This Lab Helps |
|-----------------|--------------------|
| Broken Auth | ZAP checks access-controlled paths dynamically |
| Exposed APIs | Spider finds unlinked endpoints or hidden features |
| Insecure Redirects | Actively scanned and logged in ZAP reports |
| Credential Stuffing | Auth flow testing highlights poor session handling |

---

## 🧪 How You Operationalized ZAP

- Created and reused `.context` files for session persistence
- Parsed JSON ZAP output to enforce risk gates in CI/CD
- Stored reports in S3 or GitHub for **traceability and auditability**
- Ran scans **on code change**, **after infra updates**, and **before promotion**

---

## 🧱 Best Practices for Maintaining ZAP in CI/CD

| Practice | Why It Matters |
|---------|----------------|
| 🔁 Rotate auth tokens | Prevent false positives from expired sessions |
| 🚫 Exclude static/irrelevant endpoints | Reduce scan noise and performance hits |
| 📊 Tune scan rulesets | Focus on real app risks; avoid over-alerting |
| 🧩 Reuse config across environments | Auth flows differ by stage; context helps unify scans |
| 📬 Notify security team | Send ZAP alerts via Slack, email, or SIEM hooks |

---

## 🧠 DevSecOps Concepts Reinforced

| Concept | How You Applied It |
|--------|---------------------|
| Shift-left DAST | Runs as part of every pipeline, not post-deploy |
| Security as code | ZAP config and thresholds live in Git |
| Risk-based enforcement | Builds fail on severity levels, not just scan presence |
| Continuous trust | Auth scans help verify security posture in every release |

---

By completing this week:

- You can **orchestrate live security testing** as part of delivery
- You understand **how to handle auth, sessions, and alerts** in DAST
- You’ve built a repeatable, auditable, and trusted scan pipeline

# 🧪 Week 20 – Guided Lab: Final DevSecOps Review & Demo Packaging

## 🎯 Objective

Walk through your **entire Secure Software Factory**, validating each security control, and prepare your repo and documentation for demo, portfolio, or recruiter review.

## 🧩 Step 1: Validate App Security Posture

Run a full suite of scans against your **latest deployment in `staging`**.

### 🔍 Trivy SCA & SBOM

```bash
trivy fs . --severity CRITICAL,HIGH --format table

syft . -o table
Confirm no critical CVEs exist in your Python or Node.js dependencies.

🧪 Bandit – Python Code Review
bandit -r backend/
Validate that no unsafe eval, exec, or hardcoded credentials exist.

🧪 Semgrep – Node.js + Python Pattern Checks
semgrep scan --config "p/default" --include node_modules --lang js,python
Spot insecure API usage patterns, JWT mishandling, and more.

🔐 Secrets Detection
gitleaks detect --source .
trufflehog filesystem .
Confirm no API keys or secrets were committed at any point in Git history.

🌐 ZAP DAST
docker run -v $(pwd):/zap/wrk -t owasp/zap2docker-stable zap-full-scan.py \
  -t "$STAGING_URL" \
  -c zap/context.context \
  -r zap-final-report.html
Verify endpoints, session handling, and authentication flow are secure.

🔁 Git Commit & Merge Controls
Signed commits: git log --show-signature

Protected branches for main, staging, production

Tagging for production builds: v1.0.0

Show how you ensure traceability and integrity.

📦 SBOM + Image Attestation
cosign attest <image-ref>
Link your SBOMs to Docker images with signed metadata.

🔐 CodePipeline Artifact Store
Confirm you are:

Not exposing secrets in CodeBuild logs

Rotating IAM roles/token via OIDC

Validating image provenance before promotion

🧩 Step 3: Final Threat Assessment
Walk through your system and identify:

Component	Potential Threat	Control
API Gateway	ID spoofing	IAM policy + JWT scope check
Frontend JS	XSS	CSP + output sanitization
DB access	Lateral movement	Least-privilege IAM role
Secrets in CI	Hardcoded secrets	Vault/SOPS/SealedSecrets
Service A → B	Trust abuse	ECS Service Connect namespace policy
Artifact store	Tampering	SBOM + Cosign signatures

# 🧪 Week 20 – Challenge Lab: Build + Defend Your Secure Software Factory

---

## 🎯 Goal

Prove you can manage the full DevSecOps lifecycle by:

- Automating code-to-cloud deployments
- Enforcing multiple layers of security gates

## 💻 Scenario

Your team is preparing to release **v1.0.0** of `NoteStream Pro`.

You must:
- Promote the current `staging` build to `production`
- Prove that it passed all trust gates (SCA, DAST, SBOM, Secrets, IAM)
- Simulate a realistic attack chain (e.g., leaked secret or vulnerable dependency)
- Demonstrate how your system detects, blocks, or recovers

---

### 🔁 Tag & Build

```bash

Ensure the following jobs run on the tag:

Job	Tool	Expected Output
sbom-gen	Syft	sbom.json in /dist
sca-check	Trivy, npm audit	Pass or fail on CVE threshold
sast-check	Bandit, Semgrep	Clean scan
secret-check	Gitleaks	No hardcoded secrets
image-scan	Trivy (image)	CVE severity filtered
deploy-prod	Terraform/CDK	Secure infra + ECS/K8s deploy
sign-image	Cosign	Signed Docker image

Attack	Simulate

Broken endpoint	Disable auth guard on an API route
Over-permissive IAM	Add wildcard to policy and test detection in review
Then:

Document how your tools caught it (e.g., ZAP, Gitleaks, tfsec)

Describe how a build would fail or alert you

Fix the issue and rerun pipeline to confirm secure promotion

# 🔐 Week 20 – Threat Model: NoteStream Pro

---

## 🧱 Overview

This threat model documents potential risks to the **NoteStream Pro** application and DevSecOps pipeline. It identifies:

- Key assets
- Trust boundaries
- Attack vectors
- Controls in place
- Gaps and improvements

---

## 🎯 Threat Modeling Method: STRIDE

| Category | Description |
|----------|-------------|
| S – Spoofing | Identity misrepresentation |
| T – Tampering | Malicious data alteration |
| R – Repudiation | Denial of actions without trace |
| I – Information Disclosure | Unauthorized data access |
| D – Denial of Service | Resource exhaustion |
| E – Elevation of Privilege | Gaining unauthorized permissions |

---

## 🔐 Assets to Protect

| Asset | Risk if Compromised |
|-------|---------------------|
| User credentials | Account takeover |
| Notes data (private) | Data breach |
| Secrets and tokens | Lateral movement or data exfiltration |
| SBOM/artifacts | Supply chain poisoning |
| CI/CD pipelines | Unauthorized code deployment |
| IAM roles | Privilege escalation |
| Audit logs | Lost traceability |

---

## 🔗 Trust Boundaries

| Boundary | Description |
|----------|-------------|
| User → Frontend | HTTPS access via browser/mobile |
| Frontend → Backend | Internal service comms via Service Connect |
| Backend → DB | Least-privilege RDS or NoSQL access |
| CI/CD → Infra | CodeBuild deploys to ECS/K8s |
| Developer → Git | Commits/pushes signed + validated |
| Service-to-service (microservices) | Namespace & version-aware isolation |

---

## ⚠️ Threat Scenarios and Controls

| Component | Threat | STRIDE | Control | Status |
|----------|--------|--------|---------|--------|
| Login API | Credential stuffing | S | Rate limiting, 2FA, JWT TTL | ✅ |
| Git Repo | Secrets in code | I | Gitleaks, SOPS | ✅ |
| CI Runner | Image tampering | T | SBOM, Cosign, IAM roles | ✅ |
| API Gateway | ID spoofing | S | Signed JWTs + scope validation | ✅ |
| ECS Services | Lateral movement | E | Service Connect namespace policies | ✅ |
| Terraform IAM | Wildcard policy abuse | E | tfsec, OPA policies | ✅ |
| DB | SQL injection | T/I | Parameterized queries | ✅ |
| App logs | Sensitive data logging | I | Redaction, log filters | ✅ |
| ZAP Scan | Broken auth detection | S/I | Context-based scans | ✅ |
| K8s Cluster | RBAC misconfig | E | Roles, network policies | ✅ |
| Cloud Storage | Artifact overwrite | T | Versioning, signed uploads | ✅ |
| DNS | Subdomain takeover | T/I | DNSSEC, static routing | Partial |

---

## 🔍 Visual Threat Model (Text-Based)

```txt
[User]
   |
[Frontend] ← HTTPS → [Backend API]
   |                         |
   ↓                         ↓
  LocalStorage       [Secrets Manager] → [Database]
                               ↓
                           [Audit Logs]

               DevOps Flow:
  [Dev] → [Git] → [CI/CD] → [ECS/K8s] → [Users]

Trust Boundaries:
- Public Web to Frontend
- Frontend to API (internal)
- CI/CD to Infra
- Developers to Git (signed access)
🔧 Security Enhancements for Future Work
Add WAF rules for OWASP Top 10 auto-blocking

Enable Mutual TLS for backend service calls

Rotate all secrets via Vault or Sealed Secrets on deploy

Store audit logs in immutable bucket with retention lock

Integrate ZAP findings with SIEM for alerting

✅ Summary
Threat modeling revealed:

Strong layered defenses across app and pipeline

Enforced identity, artifact trust, secrets mgmt, and risk gates

Minor gaps in DNS hygiene and backup log protections

You now operate a defensible, traceable, and trustworthy DevSecOps system.

# 🔐 Week 20 – Security Summary: End-to-End Controls for NoteStream Pro

---

## 🔐 Goal

This document summarizes the full **DevSecOps security posture** of the NoteStream Pro platform — across development, deployment, and runtime — including prevention, detection, and response capabilities.

---

## 🛠️ Security Across the Stack

| Layer | Security Measures |
|-------|--------------------|
| **Frontend** | Content Security Policy (CSP), HTTPOnly cookies, input sanitization |
| **Backend** | Parameterized queries, JWT validation, Python/Node.js linters |
| **API Gateway** | JWT scopes, rate limiting, IP filtering |
| **Database** | IAM-based access, encrypted at rest, VPC-restricted |
| **CI/CD Pipelines** | GitHub/GitLab signed commits, SBOM enforcement, secrets scanning |
| **Infrastructure as Code** | tfsec, Checkov, OPA policy-as-code validation |
| **Secrets Management** | Vault for CI/CD secrets, SOPS and Sealed Secrets for K8s |
| **Runtime (ECS/K8s)** | Service Connect namespace isolation, IAM roles for tasks, eBPF-based runtime detection |
| **DAST** | ZAP scans with auth contexts and severity filtering |
| **SCA** | Trivy, npm audit, Python pip-audit, SBOM via Syft |
| **SAST** | Semgrep, Bandit, ESLint security rules |
| **Logging & Monitoring** | CloudWatch, Loki, Prometheus, GuardDuty |
| **Image Security** | Trivy scanning, Cosign signing and attestation |
| **Threat Modeling** | STRIDE applied across app + pipeline + infra |
| **Audit Trails** | Immutable logs, S3 bucket versioning, pipeline logs stored |

---

## 🔁 DevSecOps Integration Flow

```txt
[Developer]
   ↓
[Signed Git Commit] → [CI/CD]
   → [Static Analysis (SAST)]
   → [Dependency Scan (SCA)]
   → [Secrets Scan]
   → [SBOM Generation]
   → [Build Signed Docker Image]
   → [Deploy via IaC]
   → [Runtime Monitoring (Falco/eBPF)]
   → [DAST Scan via ZAP]
   → [Logs, Alerts, and SIEM Forwarding]
🔄 Security Gates in the CI/CD Pipeline
Stage	Tool	Purpose
Pre-build	Gitleaks, Trufflehog	Secrets detection
Build	Trivy, npm audit, pip-audit	Dependency vulnerability checks
Lint/Test	Bandit, ESLint, Semgrep	Code-level issues
Package	Syft	SBOM generation
Sign	Cosign	Image trust
Deploy	Terraform, CDK	Controlled infrastructure changes
Post-deploy	ZAP, Prometheus	Live scanning & observability
✅ All security scans enforce gating with fail thresholds.

📈 Security Maturity Achieved
Category	Maturity Level
Identity & Access	Enterprise-grade IAM, Git signing, JWT
Secrets Management	Encrypted, rotated, Git-free
Vulnerability Mgmt	Shift-left SCA + runtime scanning
Observability	Metrics + logs + traces + SIEM-ready
Policy Enforcement	IaC policies + container runtime + RBAC
DevSecOps Culture	Automation-first, feedback-driven, secure by default
🎯 Security Outcomes
🚫 Prevented common web and supply chain attacks

🔍 Detected issues pre-merge and at runtime

🧾 Proved compliance and trust with traceable logs and SBOMs

🛡️ Hardened app and pipeline via code, not process

💬 "Security is not a step — it’s a discipline that flows through every commit, every service, and every deploy."

You’ve now built a production-grade, DevSecOps-enabled platform with verifiable controls.
